Congruence subgroups of small Artin and Coxeter groups
Pravin Kumar

Abstract
. Small Coxeter groups are exactly those for which the Tits representation takes integral values, which makes the study of their congruence subgroups significant. In [ 20 ] , Squier introduced a matrix representation of an Artin group defined over the ring ℤ ⁡ [ s ± , t ± ] \mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29043^{\mathchar 8710}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8710}\delimiter 84267779 of Laurent polynomials in two variables. This representation simultaneously generalises the Tits representation of the associated Coxeter groups and the reduced Burau representation of braid groups. We define small Artin groups as those for which this representation becomes integral when evaluated at s = 1 \mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721 and t = − 1 \mathchar 29044\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721 . Consequently, the study of congruence subgroups of small Artin groups extends the classical notion of congruence subgroups of braid groups, which arise from the integral reduced Burau representation. In this paper, we examine Coxeter and Artin groups that possess the congruence subgroup property and identify several of their principal congruence subgroups at small levels.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 20F36; Secondary 20F55, 19B37

1.
Introduction

Congruence subgroups of a group are defined via a choice of an integral representation into
\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
. The
level
\mathchar 29037
principal congruence subgroup

\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
of
\mathchar 28999
is then defined as the kernel of the composition map
\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84054785
. Congruence subgroups play an important role in the theory of arithmetic groups. We say that a group
\mathchar 28999
has the
congruence subgroup property
if every finite index subgroup of
\mathchar 28999
contains some principal congruence subgroup.

In the case of braid groups, the integral (unreduced) Burau representation has been used to define principal congruence subgroups. These subgroups have been studied in detail, and we refer to
[
1
,
4
,
6
,
7
,
21
]
for recent results and a survey of congruence subgroups of braid groups. Among notable results, Arnold proved that the level two principal congruence subgroups of the braid group
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}
on
\mathchar 29038
strands is the pure braid group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}

[
2
]
. Brendle and Margalit proved that the level four principal congruence subgroups of
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}
equals the kernel of the mod two abelianisation map
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29008\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785

[
7
, Main Theorem]
. Further, Kordek and Margalit determined the first rational homology of the level four principal congruence subgroup of
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}

[
14
]
.

The congruence subgroups of Coxeter groups whose Tits representation is integral have been investigated recently in
[
16
]
. It has been proved that infinite Coxeter groups with integral Tits representation which are not virtually abelian do not admit the congruence subgroup property
[
16
, Theorem 3.8]
. Further, it has been shown that the level four principal congruence subgroup of a right-angled Coxeter group identifies with its commutator subgroup
[
16
, Proposition 3.5]
.

Artin groups form a family of infinite discrete groups defined by presentations of a specific form, closely related to those of Coxeter groups. Despite their simple presentation, they remain not so well understood in many respects. The class of Artin groups is broad, encompassing braid groups, free groups, free abelian groups, as well as many more exotic examples.

In
[
20
]
, Squier introduced a matrix representation of an Artin group over the ring
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29043^{\mathchar 8710}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8710}\delimiter 84267779
of Laurent polynomials in two-variables.
The representation is referred to as the generalised Burau representation, and generalises the Burau representation of the braid group (obtained by setting
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
),
as well as the Tits representation of the corresponding Coxeter group (obtained by setting
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
). We say that an Artin group is a
small Artin group
if its generalised Burau representation is integral when we substitute
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
. This makes the study of congruence subgroups of such groups relevant, which we explore in this paper.

In this paper, we investigate the congruence subgroup property for Coxeter and Artin groups. The paper is organised as follows. In Section
2
, the basic preliminaries on Coxeter groups, Artin groups, and generalised Burau representation of Artin groups are presented. In Section
3
, we investigate the congruence subgroup problem for abstract groups and prove that a group with a finite index subgroup that surjects onto a free non-abelian group does not have the congruence subgroup property with respect to any integral representation (Proposition
3.2
). In Section
4
, we prove that a small Coxeter group which is virtually abelian admit the congruence subgroup property (Theorem
4.11
). In Section
5
, we investigate the congruence subgroup property for Artin groups with respect to integral generalised Burau representations, and prove that Artin groups associated to Coxeter graphs whose connected components are not affine do not admit the congruence subgroup property (Theorem
5.8
). In Sections
6
and
7
, we identify the principal congruence subgroups of Artin groups of small level. We prove that if
\mathchar 28993
is an Artin group associated with a spherical Coxeter graph, then
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 29015\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 29015
is the corresponding Coxeter group (Theorem
6.7
). We prove that if
\mathchar 28993
is a right-angled Artin group, then
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84267779\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 29015\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29015^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
, where
\mathchar 29015
is the corresponding right-angled Coxeter group (Theorem
7.7
).

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Coxeter groups

A group
\mathchar 29015
is called a
Coxeter group
if it admits a presentation of the form
\big\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29010\big\delimiter 86414091
, where
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28677\}
is the set of generators and

(2.1)

\mathchar 29010\mathchar 12349\big\{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}}~\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 8795\{\mathchar 561\}\mathchar 24891~\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891~\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721~\textrm{if and only if}~\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29034\big\}

is the set of defining relations. The pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
is called a
Coxeter system
. If
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 561
, there is no relation between
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}
and
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29034}
. We refer to
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
’s as
exponents
of the Coxeter system
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
. The cardinality of the set
\mathchar 29011
is called the
rank
of the Coxeter system, and we shall consider only finite rank Coxeter systems. Since Coxeter groups are completely determined by their exponents, we use matrices or graphs to encode this information.

A
Coxeter matrix
on a (finite) set
\mathchar 29011
is a symmetric matrix
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011}
, where
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 8795\{\mathchar 561\}
and satisfy
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
for all
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011
and
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
for all
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011
with
\mathchar 29033\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29034
. Each such matrix can be represented by a graph, called
Coxeter graph

\mathchar 28672
, which is a labelled simple graph with
\mathchar 29011
as its set of vertices and two vertices
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011
are joined by an edge if
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
, and such an edge is labelled with
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
if
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
.

When we refer to an abstract group
\mathchar 29015
as a Coxeter group, we mean that
\mathchar 29015
has an associated Coxeter matrix or Coxeter graph and possesses a presentation of the form (
2.1
). If the information is encoded in terms of the Coxeter matrix, we denote the Coxeter system by the pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 29011
is the set of all Coxeter generators. If the information is encoded in terms of the Coxeter graph
\mathchar 28672
, we denote the Coxeter system as
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
. Both notations are used interchangeably, depending on the context.

The Coxeter system
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
is said to be
reducible
if
\mathchar 29015\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29015_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29015_{\mathchar 28722}
, where
\mathchar 29015_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 86414091
and
\mathchar 29015_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 86414091
for some subsets
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 28722}
of
\mathchar 29011
. Otherwise, the Coxeter system is said to be
irreducible
. Equivalently, the Coxeter system is said to be irreducible if the Coxeter graph is connected.

A homomorphism
\mathchar 28960\mathchar 12346\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
between Coxeter groups is called a
graph homomorphism
if
\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 560}
or
\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
for all
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011
, and every
\mathchar 29043^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 560}
is of the form
\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785
for some
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29011
. In particular,
\mathchar 28960
is surjective.

Let
\mathchar 29015
be a Coxeter group given by a Coxeter presentation
\mathchar 29015\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29010\delimiter 86414091
, where
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28677\}
. Let
\mathchar 29014
be the real vector space spanned by the set
\{\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28677\}
. Define a symmetric bilinear form
\mathchar 28994
on
\mathchar 29014
by

\mathchar 28994\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\begin{cases}\mathchar 8704\cos\left\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 28953\over\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}}}\right\delimiter 84054785&\text{ if }\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 561\mathchar 24891\\
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721&\text{ if }\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 561\mathchar 314\end{cases}

Then, for each
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28677
, the linear map
\overline{\mathchar 28954}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29014\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29014
given by

(2.2)

\overline{\mathchar 28954}_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28994\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891

defines an automorphism of
\mathchar 29014
. The following result is a folklore
[
5
, Chapter V, Section 4]
.

Theorem 2.3
.

The map
\mathchar 28954\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29015\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
defined through
\mathchar 28954\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\overline{\mathchar 28954}_{\mathchar 29033}
is a faithful representation of
\mathchar 29015
.

The representation
\mathchar 28954\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29015\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
is called the
Tits representation
of
\mathchar 29015
. It is easy to see that Tits representation of Coxeter group is integral if and only if all expontents
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 561\}
. We say that a group is a
small Coxeter group
if it admits a Coxeter system such that each exponent
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
is either
\mathchar 561
or less than or equals to 3. For instance, symmetric groups, right-angled Coxeter groups and universal Coxeter groups are small Coxeter groups.

The classification of finite irreducible Coxeter groups consists of four families of groups:
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29038}
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
,
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
,
\mathchar 28996_{\mathchar 29038}
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
, and
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\delimiter 84054785
for
\mathchar 29040\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28725
. Additionally, there are six exceptional groups:
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28726}
,
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28727}
,
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28728}
,
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28724}
,
\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28723}
, and
\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28724}
(see the Figure
1
). We note that the labelling of vertices in Figure
1
is same as given in
[
5
]
.

Every finite Coxeter group can be expressed as a direct product of a finite number of these irreducible groups.

Figure 1.
Coxeter graphs of irreducible finite Coxeter groups.

Remark 2.4
.

Let
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785
be an irreducible Coxeter system. The group
\mathchar 29015
can be expressed as a direct sum of two non-trivial Coxeter groups if and only if
\mathchar 29015
is not isomorphic to
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
, where
\mathchar 28672\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
or
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}
(
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
)
[
18
, Theorem 3.3]
. For
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472{\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84267779
and
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779
, the following direct product decompositions holds.

\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8706\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}

and

Remark 2.5
.

From the classification of irreducible finite Coxeter groups, the Coxeter group of type
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29038}
,
\mathchar 28996_{\mathchar 29038}
, and the exceptional groups
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28726}
,
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28727}
, and
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28728}
are small Coxeter groups. Additionally, the Coxeter group of type
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28726}
and
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}
also have small Coxeter systems (cf. Remark
2.4
). However, the remaining irreducible finite Coxeter groups:
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28995_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038}
with
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
,
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
with
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28727
or
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725
, and the groups
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28724}
,
\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28723}
, and
\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28724}
, do not have any small Coxeter systems due to their rigid properties as mentioned in Remark

.

A group
\mathchar 28993

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785

(2.6)

\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12349\big\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12906\underbrace{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}}_{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\text{ terms}}\mathchar 12349\underbrace{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29034}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}}_{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\text{ terms}}\text{ for all }\mathchar 29033\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29034\text{ with }\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12604\mathchar 561\big\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 24891

3.
The congruence subgroup problem for abstract groups

Given a representation
\mathchar 28958\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
of a group
\mathchar 28999
and an integer
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, one defines the
principal congruence subgroup

\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
of level
\mathchar 29037
as the kernel of the composition

\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Notice that
\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84267779
for each divisor
\mathchar 29035
of
\mathchar 29037
. A finite index subgroup of
\mathchar 28999
containing some principal congruence subgroup is called a
congruence subgroup
. We say that the group
\mathchar 28999
has the
congruence subgroup property
, with respect to
\mathchar 28958
, if every finite index subgroup is a congruence subgroup. The
congruence subgroup problem
asks whether a group has the congruence subgroup property.

The definition of a principal congruence subgroup depends on the choice of representation. We say that two integer representations,
\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
are equivalent if there exists a matrix
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
such that
\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28993
for all
\mathchar 29031\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28999
. In this case, for any
\mathchar 29031\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28999
,
\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\delimiter 84054785
is the identity matrix modulo
\mathchar 29037
if and only if
\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\delimiter 84054785
is the identity matrix modulo
\mathchar 29037
for all
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
. Therefore, the notion of a principal congruence subgroup of a group
\mathchar 28999
is well-defined up to this equivalence relation.

Consider the representation
\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29000\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
. We can define the direct sum representation
\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
as

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29032\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\begin{\mathchar29037\mathchar29025\mathchar29044\mathchar29042\mathchar29033\mathchar29048}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\delimiter 84054785&\mathchar 28720\\
\mathchar 28720&\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\delimiter 84054785\end{\mathchar29037\mathchar29025\mathchar29044\mathchar29042\mathchar29033\mathchar29048}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

It is straightforward to see that
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
for each
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
. Indeed, if
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29032\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000
, the matrix

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29032\delimiter 84054785

are congruent to the identity modulo

. Therefore, we can conclude that
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29032\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
if and only if

.

Proposition 3.1
.

If
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785
admits the congruence subgroup property, then both
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
and
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29000\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785
admit the congruence subgroup property.

Proof.

It is enough to show that
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
has the congruence subgroup property. Let
\mathchar 29003
be a finite index subgroup of
\mathchar 28999
. Then,
\mathchar 29003\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000
is a finite index subgroup of
\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000
. Given that
\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000
has the congruence subgroup property, there exists an integer
\mathchar 29037
such that
\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12826\mathchar 29003\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29000
. By applying the projection homomorphism onto
\mathchar 28999
, we conclude that
\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12826\mathchar 29003
. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Given a group
\mathchar 28999
, the
profinite topology
or the
Krull topology
on
\mathchar 28999
is generated by a sub-basis consisting of all finite index normal subgroups of
\mathchar 28999
and their left cosets
[
22
, Section 3.7]
. The profinite topology turns
\mathchar 28999
into a topological group, and we denote its completion by
\widehat{\mathchar 28999}
. In addition, if
\mathchar 28999
is a group admitting an integral representation
\mathchar 28954
, then we can define another topology called the
congruence topology
on
\mathchar 28999
by taking all principal congruence subgroups and their left cosets as a sub-basis. Let
\overline{\mathchar 28999}
be the completion of
\mathchar 28999
under the congruence topology. Both
\widehat{\mathchar 28999}
and
\overline{\mathchar 28999}
are profinite groups, and there is a surjective homomorphism
\widehat{\mathchar 28999}\mathchar 12833\overline{\mathchar 28999}
. The
congruence kernel

\mathchar 28995_{\mathchar 28954}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\ker\delimiter 67273472\widehat{\mathchar 28999}\mathchar 12833\overline{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 84054785
is the kernel of this morphism. Then, the group
\mathchar 28999
admitting the congruence subgroup property with respect to
\mathchar 28954
is equivalent to the kernel
\mathchar 28995_{\mathchar 28954}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\delimiter 84054785
being trivial.

Equivalently, the group
\mathchar 28999
has a congruence subgroup property with respect to
\mathchar 28954
if and only if the map
\widehat{\mathchar 28999}\mathchar 12349\underleftarrow{\lim}\mathchar 28999\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29013\mathchar 12833\underleftarrow{\lim}\mathchar 28999\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
injective? where,
\mathchar 29013
ranges over all finite index normal subgroups of
\mathchar 28999
, and
\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
ranges over all principal congruence subgroup with respect to
\mathchar 28954
.

It is easy to see that
\overline{\mathchar 28999}
can be seen as subgroup of
\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\hat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}\delimiter 84054785
, indeed, we have the following inclusions:

\displaystyle\underleftarrow{\lim}\mathchar 28999\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28999\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779

\displaystyle\underleftarrow{\lim}_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}}\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 84054785

\displaystyle\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\underleftarrow{\lim}_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\hat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Therefore, the congruence subgroup property is equivalent to the question: Is the congruence map
\hat{\mathchar 28999}\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\hat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}\delimiter 84054785
injective?

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
\mathchar 28999
be a group. If
\mathchar 28999
has a finite index subgroup that surjects onto a free non-abelian group, then the pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954\delimiter 84054785
does not admit the congruence subgroup property for any representation
\mathchar 28954\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
.

Proof.

Let
\mathchar 29006
be a finite index subgroup of
\mathchar 28999
that surjects onto free non-abelian group of rank
\mathchar 29035
. The profinite completion
\widehat{\mathchar 29006}
is a subgroup of
\widehat{\mathchar 28999}
that surjects onto the profinite completion of the free group
\widehat{\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 29035}}
. Since
\widehat{\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 29035}}
is projective, there exists a subgroup
\mathchar 29003
of
\widehat{\mathchar 29006}
, that is isomorphic to
\widehat{\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 29035}}
. Thus, we have
\widehat{\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 29003\mathchar 12826\widehat{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 12826\widehat{\mathchar 28999}
, which does not admit any injective continuous homomorphism into
\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\hat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}\delimiter 84054785
for any
\mathchar 29037

[
22
, Proposition 3.8.3]
. This implies that
\widehat{\mathchar 28999}
does not admit an injective morphism into
\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\widehat{\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}}\delimiter 84054785
. In particular,
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954\delimiter 84054785
does not have congruence subgroup property.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

Let
\mathchar 28999
be a group. If
\mathchar 28999
is virtually free, then the pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954\delimiter 84054785
does not admit the congruence subgroup property for any representation
\mathchar 28954\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785
.

4.
The congruence subgroup problem for affine Coxeter groups

The Tits representation of small Coxeter group is integral which makes the study of congruence subgroups of these groups relevant. In
[
16
]
, the author, Naik, and Singh investigate which small Coxeter groups have the congruence subgroup property. If
\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}
denote a property of groups, then a group is said to be virtually
\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}
if it has a finite index subgroup with property
\mathcal{\mathchar 29008}
.

Theorem 4.1
.

[
16
, Theorem 3.8]

An infinite small Coxeter group which is not virtually abelian does not admit the congruence subgroup property with respect to the Tits representation of its corresponding small Coxeter system.

In this section, we will prove that an infinite small Coxeter group which is virtually abelian does have the congruence subgroup property. This will provide a complete answer to the congruence subgroup problem for small Coxeter groups with respect to Tits representation. In terms of Coxeter graph, a Coxeter group is virtually abelian if and only if each connected components of its Coxeter graph is either spherical or affine.

The classification of irreducible affine Coxeter groups consists of four families of groups:
\tilde{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28994}_{\mathchar 29038}
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28995}_{\mathchar 29038}
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, and
\tilde{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 29038}
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
. Additionally, there are six groups:
\tilde{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 28721}
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28726}
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28727}
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28728}
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28724}
, and
\tilde{\mathchar 28999}_{\mathchar 28722}
.

Remark 4.2
.

Among the classification of irreducible affine Coxeter groups, the Coxeter group of types
\tilde{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}\ \delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 29038}\ \delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28726}\mathchar 24891\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28727}\mathchar 24891\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28728}
and
\tilde{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 28721}
are small Coxeter groups. Affine (Weyl) Coxeter groups are strongly rigid
[
9
, Main Theorem]
and hence other irreducible affine Coxeter groups
\tilde{\mathchar 28994}_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\tilde{\mathchar 28995}_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\tilde{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 24891
and
\widetilde{\mathchar 28999}_{\mathchar 28722}
are not small.

Proposition 4.4
.

If
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779
is a irreducible affine Coxeter group and
\mathchar 29000
is a normal subgroup of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779
, then one of the following is true:

(1)

\mathchar 29000
is a
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
-invariant subgroup of
\mathchar 29009
;

(2)

\mathchar 29000
is an extension of a
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
-invariant subgroup
\mathchar 29004
of
\mathchar 29009
containing
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29009
by the center of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
;

(3)

There exists a graph homomorphism
\mathchar 28960\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779
to a finite Coxeter group
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779
, and a subgroup
\mathchar 29018
of the centre of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779
, such that
\mathchar 29000
is the kernel of the homomorphism
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29018
induced by
\mathchar 28960
.

Proposition 4.6
.

If
\tilde{\mathchar 28672}
is an irreducible affine small Coxeter graph with the corresponding spherical Coxeter graph
\mathchar 28672
, then the principal congruence subgroup
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785
is a
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
-invariant subgroup of the translation subgroup
\mathchar 29009
. When
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
,
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
is an extension of a
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
-invariant subgroup
\mathchar 29004
of
\mathchar 29009
containing
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29009
by the center of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
.

Proof.

The Coxeter group of type
\tilde{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 29038}\ \delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28996}_{\mathchar 29038}\ \delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28726}
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28727}
,
\tilde{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28728}
and
\tilde{\mathchar 28993}_{\mathchar 28721}
are the only irreducible affine small Coxeter group. By Remark
4.3
, the non-trivial element (if it exists) of the center of the corresponding spherical Coxeter group is in
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
if and only if
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
. By Proposition
4.4
, it is enough to show that
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
is not the kernel of homomorphism of the form
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\xrightarrow{\mathchar 29041}\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 29041
is a graph homomorphism and
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779
is finite Coxeter group.

By definition, any graph homomorphism from an irreducible affine Coxeter graph to a spherical Coxeter graph should map a generator to the identity element or map some pairs of generator to the same generator in
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28678\delimiter 84267779
.

When
\tilde{\mathchar 28672}
is an irreducible affine Coxeter graphs, using the Remark
4.5
, the image of each generator
\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29034}
under the Tits representation
\mathchar 28954
atleast one non-diagonal entry of
\mathchar 29034
-th row is equal to
\mathchar 28721
. Thus, we have

\mathchar 28954\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29001\mod\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

For
\mathchar 29033\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29034
, the image of
\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29034}
under
\mathchar 28954
is a matrix with the
\mathchar 29033
-th and
\mathchar 29034
-th row possibly containing non-zero entries with
\mathchar 29034
-th row has atleast one non-diagonal entry equal to
\mathchar 28721
. Thus, we have

\mathchar 28954\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29001\mod\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Thus, for all
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
,
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\tilde{\mathchar 28672}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
is not the kernel of any graph homomorphism. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

If
\mathchar 29015
is a small Coxeter group which is virtually abelian, then the level
\mathchar 29037
principal congruence subgroups of
\mathchar 29015
is a free abelian group for
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
.

Remark 4.8
.

Any finite index subgroup of a free abelian group of rank
\mathchar 29038
is also a free abelian group of the same rank
\mathchar 29038
.

Let

be an irreducible affine small Coxeter graph with the corresponding spherical Coxeter graph

. Every

and

Theorem 4.11
.

An infinite small Coxeter group which is virtually abelian admits the congruence subgroup property with respect to the Tits representation of its corresponding small Coxeter system.

Proof.

A Coxeter group is virtually abelian if and only if each connected components of its Coxeter graph is either spherical or affine. Let

\mathchar 28672
which are of affine type, and let
\mathchar 28672_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29032}}
be the spherical part of
\mathchar 28672
. Then the Coxeter group decomposes as:

5.
Congruence subgroup property for Artin groups

Let
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
be the Artin group associated with a Coxeter graph
\mathchar 28672
and let
\mathchar 28955\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
be its generalised Burau representation. By evaluating this representation at
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
, we obtain another representation
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 29038
is the rank of corresponding Coxeter system.

Example 5.1
.

If
\mathchar 28672
is graph on
\mathchar 29038
vertices without any edges, then
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is a free-abelian group and the representation
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
is a trivial representation.

In the case of the braid group, the representation
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
is a symplectic representation (up to conjugacy). The kernel of this symplectic representation is called
braid Torelli group
. This normal subgroup is a non-trivial subgroup of braid groups for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
and is an infinite index subgroup for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
. These subgroups have been well-studied (see
[
8
]
).

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\mathchar 28672
be a graph on
\mathchar 29038
vertices with no vertex of degree zero. If
\mathchar 29026\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
, then
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
is a block matrix with each block either
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29037}
or
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29037}
, where
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29037}
is the identity matrix of order
\mathchar 29037
. In particular, if each connected component of
\mathchar 28672
contains an odd number of vertices, then
\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12826\ker\delimiter 67273472\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
.

Proof.

Let
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is an Artin group with Artin generators
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}
. Under
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
, the image of each generator
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29034}
is a matrix with
\mathchar 28721
in the diagonal entries, arbitrary entries in the
\mathchar 29034
-th row, and zero in all non-diagonal entries outside the
\mathchar 29034
-th row. Since
\mathchar 28672
has no vertices of degree zero, the
\mathchar 29034
-th row of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
contains atleast one non-zero non-diagonal entry.

Let
\mathchar 29026\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
. By comparing the matrices
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29026\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
and
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
, along with the fact that
\mathchar 28672
has no vertices of degree zero, we can conclude that all non-diagonal entries of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
are
\mathchar 28720
and the
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785
-th and
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\delimiter 84054785
-th entries of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
are same if
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28722
. Furthermore, if
\mathchar 28672
is connected, then the matrix
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
is a scalar matrix. Since the determinant of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
is equal to
\mathchar 28721
, it follows that the scalar is a real
\mathchar 29038
-th root of unity. Therefore,
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
is either
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29038}
or
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29038}
when
\mathchar 29038
is even, and
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785
is
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29038}
when
\mathchar 29038
is odd. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

If
\mathchar 28672\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28724}
, then
\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28724}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28673\delimiter 86414091
, but
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28724}
.

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
\mathchar 28672
be a graph on
\mathchar 29038
vertices with no vertices of degree zero. If the group
\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
is non-trivial, then the kernel of the representation
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
is a non-trivial normal subgroup of
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
. If
\mathchar 28672
is a spherical Coxeter graph with at least two vertices, then the representation
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
is not faithful.

Proof.

If
\mathchar 29026\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
, then by Proposition
5.2
, either
\mathchar 29026
or
\mathchar 29026^{\mathchar 28722}
is in the kernel of the representation
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
. If
\mathchar 28672
is a spherical Coxeter graph with at least two vertices, then it is easy to see that
\mathchar 28672
satisfies the hypothesis of Proposition
5.2
. This completes the proof of the corollary.
∎

For convenience of terminology, we say that a group is a
small Artin group
if it admits a Coxeter system such that each exponent
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
is either
\mathchar 561
or less than or equal to 3. For example, braid groups and right-angled Artin groups are small Artin groups. The following is an immediate observation for small Artin groups.

Lemma 5.5
.

The generalised Burau representation of an Artin group evaluated at
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
is integral if and only if it is a small Artin group.

Proof.

Let
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
be a Artin group and
\mathchar 28955\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
given by
\mathchar 28955\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\overline{\mathchar 28955}_{\mathchar 29033}
be its generalised Burau representation. Clearly, the entries of the matrix of
\overline{\mathchar 28955}_{\mathchar 29033}
lie in
\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8710\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 86414091\}
. It follows that
\mathchar 8710\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8710\mathchar 28722\cos\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 28953\over\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}}}\delimiter 84054785
is an integer if and only if
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723
or
{\mathchar 561}
.
∎

Thus, the matrix representation of a small Artin group is integral when we substitute
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
and in this case, we call
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
as
integral generalised Burau representation
. This makes the study of congruence subgroups of such groups relevant.

Remark 5.6
.

Let
\mathchar 28672
and
\mathchar 28682
be two spherical type Coxeter graphs. If
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is isomorphic to
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28682\delimiter 84267779
, then
\mathchar 28672\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28682

[
19
, Theorem 1.1]
. Futher, it can be possibly that a nonspherical type Artin group can be isomorphic to a spherical type Artin group. Thus, if
\mathchar 28672
is spherical, then
\mathchar 28672
admits a small spherical Coxeter system if and only if
\mathchar 28672\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28996_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28726}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28727}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28728}
and
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28726}
.

Remark 5.7
.

Let
\mathchar 28672
be a graph with two connected components
\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28722}
. Then, we have
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84267779
and
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84267779
. It is easy to see that the Tits representation of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is the direct sum of the Tits representations of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779
and
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84267779
. Similarly, the generalised Burau representation of
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is the direct sum of the generalised Burau representations of
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779
and
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84267779
.

The braid group
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28723}
and affine Artin group of type
\tilde{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 28721}}
are virtually free and hence they do not possess congruence subgroup property with respect to any integral representations. However, almost all Artin groups are not virtually-free groups. For instance, if
\mathchar 28672
is non-complete graph, then
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is not virtually-free groups. This is because
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
contains a subgroup isomorphic to
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 28722}
as subgroup and for a virtually free group, every finitely generated subgroup of must also be virtually free and
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 28722}
is not virtually free.

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
\mathchar 28672
be a small Coxeter graph. If
\mathchar 28672
has a connected component that is not an affine Coxeter graph, then
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
does not admit the congruence subgroup property.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.1
and Remark
5.7
, it is enough to show that for any irreducible non-affine Coxeter graph
\mathchar 28672
, the group
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
do not have congruence subgroup property.

If
\mathchar 28672
is spherical and if
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
has congruence subgroup property, then every finite index subgroup contains the kernel of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
and by Corollary
5.4
, the intersection of all finite index subgroups is non-trivial subgroup, which contradicts the fact that
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is a residually finite group
[
10
,
11
,
15
]
.
Let us assume that
\mathchar 28672
is neither spherical nor Affine type. In this case, the corresponding Coxeter group
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is an infinite group which is not virtually abelian. By
[
12
, Theorem II]
,
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
has a finite index subgroup
\mathchar 29006
which surjects onto a free group of rank two. The subgroup
\mathchar 28953^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29006\delimiter 84054785
is finite index in
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
which surjects onto a non-abelian free group. By Proposition
3.2
, it follows that
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
do not have congruence subgroup property. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

We note that the only cases excluded in Theorem
5.8
are the small Coxeter graphs whose connected components are all of affine types. Here, an irreducible Artin group
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is said to be affine if
\mathchar 28672
is an irreducible affine Coxeter graphs.

6.
Level two principal congruence subgroups

In view of Lemma
5.5
, the generalised Burau representation of a small Artin group, when evaluating at
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
, is integral, and hence it is interesting to explore its (principal) congruence subgroups. Let
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
be a small Artin group with
\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29014\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29038
, where
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
. For each
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, let

\tilde{\mathchar 28955}_{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 84054785

be the composition of

Question 6.1
.

For each
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, the subgroup
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779

\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
?

Let

be a small Artin group with Artin generators
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}
, where

. Fixing the ordered basis

for the real vector space

, by definition of

, the Artin group

acts on a vector space

with basis

Our first observation is the following result.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\mathchar 28672
be a small Coxeter graph. Then, for each integer
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, we have

\delimiter 69632778\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 29037}~\mathchar 12906~\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 86414091_{\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779}~\unlhd~\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779

In particular, if
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
, then the pure Artin group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is contained in
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
.

Proof.

When
\mathchar 28672
is a small Coxeter graph, then for each
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
,
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 86414091
is an integer. Using the definition of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
, we have

\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29037\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 314

Reduce modulo
\mathchar 29037
, we get

\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 29034}\mod\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84054785\quad\text{ for all }\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 314

Thus, the matrix representation of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
on
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 29037}
is the identity matrix when reduced modulo
\mathchar 29037
. This implies that the normal closure
\delimiter 69632778\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12826\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84267779
.

In particular, when
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
, the normal closure of
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}^{\mathchar 28722}
in
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is the pure artin group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
. Thus, we have
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12826\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

5.8
, for some integer

, the containment in Proposition

is a strict containment. Indeed, if

, then for any finite index subgroup

of

\mathchar 29033
, there exist

such that
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000
. Set
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4945\displaylimits\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29033}
. Then
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000
for each

, and

Lemma 6.5
.

If
\mathchar 28672
is not a right-angled small Coxeter graph, then the quotient
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
is non-abelian. In particular,
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
. It is sufficient to show that
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mod\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
. Indeed, we have:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785

\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28721}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785

\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\tilde{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785

\displaystyle\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029_{\mathchar 28721}

Thus, when reduced modulo
\mathchar 28722
, we see that
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\tilde{\mathchar 28955}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
. Consequently, the image of
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}
in the quotient
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
do not commutes. This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 6.7
.

If
\mathchar 28672
is a spherical Coxeter graph, then
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\ker\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.2
and Lemma
6.5
, the group
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
is a finite non-abelian quotient of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
.

When
\mathchar 28672
is of type
\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29038}
or
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28726}
, the smallest non-abelian quotient of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
itself. Thus, we have
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29008\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
when
\mathchar 28672\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29038}
or
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28726}
.

When
\mathchar 28672\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28727}
or
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28728}
, the non-abelian quotient of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
is either
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
or
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
. By Corollary
5.4
, a lift (the Garside element) of the longest element of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779
lies in the kernel of
\tilde{\mathchar 28955}
. Thus, we have
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
. This shows that
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\ker\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29018\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
when
\mathchar 28672\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28727}
or
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28728}
.

When

\mathchar 28960\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28996_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67482370{\mathchar 28993_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84267779\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
\mathchar 28672
be a Coxeter graph and let
\mathchar 29035
be an odd integer. Then

\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84267779\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 314

Proof.

Let
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038}
be the Artin generators. By Proposition
6.2
, we have
\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29036\delimiter 84267779
for all
\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
. Let
\mathchar 29041\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
be the quotient map. When restricted to
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84267779
, the map
\mathchar 29041
is surjective because
\mathchar 29041\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
.
Since
\mathchar 29035
is odd, the kernel of this restricted homomorphism is
\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 8796\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28672\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84267779
.Therefore, by the first isomorphism theorem, the proposition follows.
∎

.

\mathchar 28939\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\begin{cases}~~\mathchar 28720\mod\mathchar 28722&\text{if }\mathchar 29033\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\\
